An INT3 breakpoint at 0x665546 would fire only if the instruction at 0x665545 (or a longer one spanning it) executes. If execution went through the mov [rbp+0x648], rdi instruction, RIP would be 0x665546 when the INT3 is hit (INT3 exception reports RIP = address of INT3 byte, and since 0x665546 is the second byte of the instruction, the CPU hits the INT3 at that byte → RIP 0x665546).

Hmm wait, that's wrong. When the CPU executes a multi-byte instruction, it decodes the whole instruction and executes it atomically — it does NOT fetch byte-by-byte in a way that would trigger an INT3 mid-instruction... Actually, it DOES: x86 fetches instructions and if a byte within the instruction's bytes contains an INT3 (0xCC) that was patched in by a debugger, the decode... hmm. Actually no. When x64dbg sets a software breakpoint, it overwrites the first byte of the target instruction with 0xCC. If you put 0xCC in the MIDDLE of an instruction, then when the CPU tries to execute that instruction, the decode stage fetches all the bytes; an 0xCC byte in the instruction stream while decoding → #BP exception. Yes — actually for INT3 in the middle of an instruction, Intel says the #BP is raised when the byte is encountered during decoding, with RIP pointing at the 0xCC byte. This is the classic way people create "unreachable byte" traps. So yes, an INT3 at byte 2 of a 7-byte instruction WOULD fire with RIP = that byte's address when the instruction is decoded/executed.

OK so the trace DID fire DAILY_GATE at raw 0x665546 (mid-instruction of mov qword ptr [rbp+0x648], rdi). That's... a function in region A, not the DailyUpdate gate. Let me write a proper pattern scanner: iterate over all possible instruction START positions? That's expensive but doable: for each byte position p (aligned to real instruction boundaries unknowable), check if bytes p..p+4 match E8 disp32 and p+5+disp == target. The false-positive rate for a specific target is low (1/4G), so a full scan over all byte positions for the specific disp is feasible: just search for the exact 5-byte sequence E8 + disp32(computed) for each known target. That finds the call instruction bytes regardless of alignment.

disp for call to target from VA of call site p: disp = target - (p+5). I can compute for each p the needed disp and compare to bytes at p+1..p+4. Simple: iterate p from 0 to len-5, read d = int32(bytes[p+1:p+5]), if bytes[p]==0xE8 and (VA_BASE+p+5+d) == target → hit. That's O(n) per target.

Summary of what the trace proves:

    The x64dbg log (x64dbg logs.txt) contains the V8 clean trace from BOTH the warm session (resign→load, feats work) and the cold session (quit→relaunch→load, feats 0). The log is "incomplete" only in that it ends right after the second CALC_RESULT in the cold session — no later day-ticks are captured (the "after one in-game day" question remains open).

    Sequence in both sessions:
        DAILY_GATE fires (bp at CK2game.exe+666146, which is 0x1000 BEFORE the documented gate at raw 0x666546/VA 0x140667146 — it lands mid-instruction inside the same big function F1, so it still signals "DailyUpdate ran"; the intended je byte is at +667146).
        UPDATE_ENTRY fires → UpdateFeatProgress called (raw 0x666550/VA 0x140667150 in DailyUpdate, or raw 0x7856f2/VA 0x1407862f2 in the RESTORE function).
        UpdateFeatProgress (VA 0x1407b8e60) calls CalcShouldTrackFeatProgress (VA 0x1407b8450) as its FIRST action (call at 0x1407b8e81; test al,al at 0x1407b8e86 = CALC_RESULT bp; je → bail to 0x1407b932e leaving the in-game counters untouched).
        RULER_INFO_CHECK (VA 0x1407b848d): RAX non-null in BOTH sessions → the current-ruler-info check passes both times.
        CALC_RETURN_PATH (VA 0x1407b8519, the success-path ret): fired in BOTH sessions → all intermediate gates passed (expiry, mode bytes +0x500/+0x501, [singleton+0x60]==0, [singleton+0x65]!=0) and the code reached the FINAL check.
        CALC_FAIL_PATH (VA 0x1407b84f8): NEVER fired → none of the intermediate fail branches were taken.
        CALC_RESULT: warm rax=1, cold rax=0 → the FINAL gate — 0x1400af690 (the achievement-eligibility helper, called at 0x1407b8506 with rcx=the 0x1400af050 singleton, dl=1, r8=0) — returned 1 warm, 0 cold.

    Conclusion: V8's premise is disproven. With both V8 edits active (the trace was captured on the V8 exe — the BAT hard-gates the V8 hash and the user's follow-up ran it), the cold-load failure is NOT at the two IsActiveForPlaythrough gates V8 patched. Every gate before the final one passes on cold load. The failure is at 0x1400af690 (and/or in the flags it reads: the 0x1400af050 singleton's +0x61/+0x62/+0x63/+0x64 bytes, or the 0x14072d540 sub-query, or the game-state global at [rip+0x1633b3b]). This is why V8 "still didn't work".

    Secondary findings:
        0x1400af050 = lazy singleton getter (0x68-byte object; first-init: dword at +0x60 = 0x00010001 → bytes +0x60=0,+0x61=1,+0x62=0,+0x63=1; word at +0x64 = 0). CalcShouldTrackFeatProgress requires +0x60==0 and +0x65!=0 before calling 0x1400af690; 0x1400af690 returns 1 iff [this+0x64]!=0, or (after the 0x14072d540 query passes) +0x61 && +0x63 && +0x62 all nonzero.
        The DAILY_GATE bp offset in the trace script is off by 0x1000 (666146 vs 667146) — harmless (same function), but worth fixing.
        0x14072d540 = a per-entry validation loop (looks up each entry's two ids via 0x14072c6c0/0x14072d010 and checks a "enabled" byte at +0x58/+0x59) — plausibly DLC/achievement-requirement checks; returns 1 only if all entries pass.

Hmm wait — actually, hold on. Let me reconsider whether +0x62 is really the deciding flag, because there's a subtlety in 0x1400af690: the +0x61/+0x63/+0x62 check is ONLY reached if the 0x14072d540 sub-query passed. From init: +0x61=1, +0x63=1, +0x62=0. So the check cmp [rdi+0x61],0; je → esi=0; cmp [rdi+0x63],0; je; cmp [rdi+0x62],0; je → since +0x62=0 (if not set), esi=0 → return 0. For return 1: +0x62 must be 1 (assuming +0x61/+0x63 already 1 from init).

But WAIT — there's also the earlier branch: cmp byte ptr [rcx+0x64], 0; je 0x1400af6be — if +0x64 != 0 → return 1 WITHOUT any of that. So if something set +0x64=1, it returns 1 too. So the story is coherent:

Cold-load feat-0 root cause (trace + disassembly):

    CalcShouldTrackFeatProgress (VA 0x1407b8450) is the gate for UpdateFeatProgress (VA 0x1407b8e60).
    Its final check calls 0x1400af690, which returns "achievement-eligibility" for the session. That returns 1 only if singleton+0x62 == 1 (the "you're allowed to earn achievements in this playthrough" flag).
    singleton+0x62 is set to 1 in exactly ONE place: VA 0x1407688b3, inside function VA 0x140768716, which reads recommended_dlc_list/YOUKICKED/war_shield(?) from the game state and is invoked (indirectly — no direct callers) during session setup.
    On a warm resign→load, the process keeps the singleton state (+0x62 already 1) → eligibility passes → feats populate.
    On a cold quit→relaunch→load, the setup dispatch that calls 0x140768716 doesn't run (or runs before the save's variables are restored / without the flags), leaving +0x62=0 → 0x1400af690 returns 0 → CalcShouldTrackFeatProgress returns 0 → UpdateFeatProgress bails → in-game feats stay 0. Main menu unaffected (reads the cache file directly).

V9 candidate options:

    Patch the +0x62 flag to be set unconditionally on load — e.g., NOP the conditional parts of function B... risky without knowing the call site. BUT — practical consideration: patching options exist for either case:

    If 0x14072d540 is the failing sub-query: it's a per-entry DLC/achievement requirement loop — cold-load likely means the achievements/DLC DB isn't populated when the first UpdateFeatProgress runs, OR one of the required DLC flags reads 0. Wait the sequence: cmp byte ptr [rdi+0x61],0; je 0x1400af795(→esi=0); cmp [rdi+0x63],0; je 0x1400af795; cmp [rdi+0x62],0; je 0x1400af797; ...0x1400af795: xor esi,esi; 0x1400af797: movzx eax,sil; ret. Hmm, the last je 0x1400af797 jumps PAST the xor esi,esi (esi stays 1 → return 1). Patching that je to NOP → falls into xor esi,esi → return 0. Opposite! Let me re-read:

0x1400af783  cmp byte ptr [rdi+0x61], 0

  raw 0x000aeb83  VA 0x00000001400af783  cmp byte ptr [rdi + 0x61], 0

Hmm wait, but let me re-examine the branch order:

cmp [rdi+0x61], 0 ; je FAIL(esi=0)
cmp [rdi+0x63], 0 ; je FAIL(esi=0)
cmp [rdi+0x62], 0 ; je SUCCESS(esi stays 1)  ← +0x62==0 → SUCCESS

So: +0x61 and +0x63 must be nonzero; +0x62 must be ZERO. +0x62=1 = "blocked". OK — so my "+0x62 is the eligibility flag" reading was inverted. The writer at 0x1407688b3 sets the BLOCKED flag (probably when YOUKICKED/etc. indicate cheating)!

Then what makes 0x1400af690 return 0 in the cold session?? If the singleton is in its INITIAL state (+0x62=0), this check passes → return 1 (if 0x14072d540 passed). So the cold-session 0 must come from: (a) 0x14072d540 returning 0 (the sub-query), or (b) +0x61 or +0x63 == 0 (but init sets them to 1; would something clear them? (d) the r8b branch at 0x1400af6c5: test r8b,r8b; je 0x1400af6e8 — r8b=0 at our call site → skip. (not the cause) (e) the dl branch: dl=1 → the 0x14072d540 path.

    0x14072d540 returned 0, or
    one of +0x61/+0x63 was 0 (unlikely — they're set at first alloc to 1 and nothing else writes them except... the only +0x61=1 writers found: raw 0x297149, 0x471154, 0x758225, 0x9880d1, 0x988148 — but those write to [rax+0x61] where rax may be a DIFFERENT object (e.g., not the 0x1400af050 singleton — 0x9880d1/0x988148 are in a different module region...). Let me draft the analysis doc content:
V8 clean feat-path trace — verdict (2026-08-30)
TL;DR

The V8 clean trace (x64dbg logs.txt) captured BOTH the warm session (feats OK) and the cold session (feats 0) on the V8 executable. It proves:

V8's hypothesis is disproven. With both V8 edits in place, after a cold launch→load the failure is NOT at the two IsActiveForPlaythrough gates V8 patches. Every gate before the final one PASSES on cold load (ruler-info non-null, expiry OK, mode bytes +0x500/+0x501 OK, singleton +0x60=0 and +0x65≠0). The cold-session "0" comes from the FINAL check in CalcShouldTrackFeatProgress — the call to 0x1400af690 (achievement-eligibility helper) at 0x1407b8506 — which returns 1 in the warm session and 0 in the cold session.

This is exactly why V8 "still didn't work": it bypassed gates that were already passing.
What the log contains

    3× "CLEAN V8 TRACE ARMED" (script re-armed)
    Warm session (Pavao 470001 load): DAILY_GATE → UPDATE_ENTRY → RULER_INFO_CHECK (rax non-null) → CALC_RETURN_PATH rax=1 → CALC_RESULT rax=1; then RESTORE_GATE → same → rax=1. (feats populated)
    Cold session (relaunch + load, "Creating title b_dyn_... on date 1278.1.1"): DAILY_GATE → UPDATE_ENTRY → RULER_INFO_CHECK (rax non-null) → CALC_RETURN_PATH rax=0 → CALC_RESULT rax=0; RESTORE_GATE → same → rax=0. (feats stay 0)
    CALC_FAIL_PATH never fired in either session._

The chain (Windows 3.3.3 May-2020, VA = raw + 0x140000c00)

[table of addresses]
Why V8 cannot fix it

[the two V8 edits + trace evidence]
What actually decides warm vs cold

    0x1400af690 (raw 0xaea90) logic walk-through:
        +0x64 ≠ 0 → return 1

So the flow is:

    If global flag VA 0x1416e3163 != 0 → return 1 immediately.
    IsActiveForPlaythrough() → if 0 → return 0. V8 bypasses this with eb 05.
    ruler info via vtable → if null → return 0.
    Date check: [rbx+0x20] >= date → else return 0.
    Mode bytes [rax+0x501] / [rax+0x500] → if both 0 → return 0.
    Singleton [+0x60] must be 0 → else return 0.
    Singleton [+0x65] must be != 0 → else return 0.
    Final gate 0x1400af690(singleton, dl=1, r8d=0) → setne al.

In the cold trace: RULER_INFO_CHECK passed (rax=...7050, zf=0), CALC_RETURN_PATH al=0 → the final gate returned 0. In the warm trace: CALC_RETURN_PATH al=1.

So the cold failure = final gate 0x1400af690 returned 0, and the gate's body says (from the session memory):

    [this+0x64]!=0 → return true immediately
    global [rip+0x1633b3b] exists + r8b path → possible early false (not our path)
    with dl=1, call 0x14072d540 on global+0x530 (or allocated object); if returns 0 → gate returns 0
    then return true iff [this+0x61]!=0 and [this+0x63]!=0

The singleton defaults: +0x60=0, +0x61=1, +0x62=0, +0x63=1, +0x64=0, +0x65=0. So the +0x61/+0x63 conditions are satisfied by default. Therefore the cold failure must come from 0x14072d540 returning 0.

0x14072d540 (all-entries-visible loop): walks [rcx+0x10..0x18] (stride 8); each element is a 2-dword key; lookup via 0x14072c6c0(type, 0) in the global container at [rip+0xd237e9] → then 0x14072d010(id) in that container's sub-list → ANDs byte [result+0x58] (or [+0x59] if [rcx+0x46]!=0). Empty vector → true.

So on cold load, either:

    the key vector at [rcx+0x10..0x18] is non-empty, and one of the lookups fails (returns NULL → flag 0 → loop returns 0), or
    the linked entries' +0x58/+0x59 flag bytes are 0.

The +0x58 flag is probably "is this feature available/unlocked". On a cold load, the features' "unlocked/available" flags aren't set yet at the time UpdateFeatProgress runs (ordering issue: the DB is populated later, or the flags are set by something that only happens in-session).

The trace showed CALC_RETURN_PATH al=0 on cold and al=1 on warm — meaning in both cases the flow reached the final gate call at 0x7b8506, and the gate returned 0 on cold / 1 on warm. Since CALC_RETURN_PATH is only reachable via the [+0x65]!=0 path (jne at 0x7b84eb), we know:

    [singleton+0x60] == 0 ✓ (otherwise returned 0 at 0x7b84e0 → CALC_FAIL_PATH, which never fired)
    [singleton+0x65] != 0 ✓ (otherwise returned 0 at 0x7b84ed → CALC_FAIL_PATH, which never fired)

The trace: CALC_FAIL_PATH (0x1407b84f8) NEVER fired in the cold burst (grep 0 hits). CALC_RETURN_PATH (0x1407b8519) fired with al=0. Design of RUN_APPLY_CK2_MJ_V9.bat (goto style, CRLF):
bat

@echo off
setlocal
title CK2 Monarch's Journey - V9 Apply (double-click)
rem ================================================================
rem  Double-click this file. It finds CK2game.exe, applies the V9
rem  patch, and keeps this window open so you can read the result.
rem  Requirements: patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1 in the same folder.
rem ================================================================
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "EXE=%~1"
rem --- 1) use the file dragged onto this window ---
if defined EXE goto :found
rem --- 2) look in the usual places ---
if exist "C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "C:\Program Files\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Program Files\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
rem --- 3) let the user pick the file ---
echo I could not find CK2game.exe automatically.
echo Please choose it with the file window that opens now.
echo (If nothing opens, drag CK2game.exe onto this BAT instead.)
echo.
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%P in (`powershell -NoProfile -Command "Add-Type -AssemblyName System.Windows.Forms; $f=New-Object System.Windows.Forms.OpenFileDialog; $f.Filter='CK2game.exe|CK2game.exe'; $f.Title='Select your CK2game.exe'; if($f.ShowDialog() -eq [System.Windows.Forms.DialogResult]::OK){$f.FileName}"`) do set "EXE=%%P"
if defined EXE goto :found
echo No file was selected. Nothing was changed.
pause
exit /b 1
:found
if not exist "%EXE%" goto :badpath
echo Found: %EXE%
goto :check_ps1
:badpath
echo ERROR: "%EXE%" does not exist.
pause
exit /b 1
:check_ps1
if exist "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" goto :apply
echo ERROR: patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1 must be in the same folder as this BAT.
pause
exit /b 1
:apply
echo.
echo This will change that file to V9. A backup is made automatically.
echo.
pause
powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" Apply "%EXE%"
if errorlevel 1 goto :failed
echo.
echo ============================================================
echo V9 FEAT-REHYDRATION PATCH COMPLETE
echo Executable: %EXE%
echo Expected V9 SHA-256:
echo 61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687
echo.
echo Test: quit CK2 completely, relaunch, Load/Continue, check feats.
echo ============================================================
pause
exit /b 0
:failed
echo.
echo PATCH FAILED. Nothing was changed.
pause
exit /b 1

Wait — issue: for /f with a backtick-quoted powershell command — inside a bat, backticks are literal (good, powershell uses them as escape char, not needed here). The command has ; and spaces and () — with usebackq and the whole command in backticks, cmd passes it to the shell... Actually for /f ... in (\command`)executes the command viacmd /c. Quoting: the backtick region content is passed to cmd as a single command line. The $f.Filter='CK2game.exe|CK2game.exe'contains|— a pipe character! Inside the backtick-quoted for /f command,|would be interpreted by cmd as a pipe!! That's a bug. I need to avoid|in the filter. Use$f.Filter='Executable (.exe)|.exe'— same problem. Workaround: set the filter without pipe:$f.Filter='CK2game.exe'? OpenFileDialog requires "description|pattern" format... Actually if Filter is malformed, it may throw. Better: skip the filter entirely or use $f.Filter = 'CK2game.exe'; $f.FilterIndex = 1— hmm, without|` the dialog may show nothing.

Alternative: escape the pipe as ^| inside the for command: $f.Filter='Executable (*.exe)^|*.exe' — cmd would pass | to powershell after unescaping? In the backtick-quoted command context, ^ escapes work. Let me think: for /f "usebackq delims=" %%P in (\command with ^| inside`)— cmd parses the command line, sees backticks as the command delimiter, and inside,^|→ cmd treats^|as literal|passed to the child. Yes, that works:^|inside the backticked command passes a literal|`. This is a well-known pattern. But it's getting fragile...

Simpler: avoid OpenFileDialog entirely and just ask them to type the path? Non-technical user can't type paths well.

Alternative: use a different picker approach with PowerShell but written to a temp .ps1 file? Overkill.

Alternative: keep the picker but simplify: no filter:

Now — another subtlety with for /f: if the PowerShell output contains trailing CR, delims= handles it (the "usebackq delims=" with empty delims means no delimiter stripping... actually delims= (empty) means no delimiters, so the whole line is taken, and trailing \r is stripped by for /f automatically). Also important: after :found etc, endlocal? Now B. Rewrite APPLY_CK2_MJ_V9.bat in goto style (same behavior, drag-drop only):
bat

@echo off
setlocal
title Apply CK2 Monarch's Journey V9 Feat-Rehydration Fix
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "EXE=%~1"

echo.
pause

:haveexe

if exist "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" goto :runpatch
echo ERROR: patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1 is not beside this BAT file.
pause
exit /b 1
:badexe
echo ERROR: "%EXE%" does not exist.
pause
exit /b 1
:runpatch
powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" Apply "%EXE%"
if errorlevel 1 goto :patchfail
echo.
if exist "%~dp1gfx\monarchs" goto :payloadok
echo ERROR: Payload not found at "%~dp1gfx\monarchs"
pause
exit /b 1
:payloadok
powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command "$h=(Get-FileHash -LiteralPath '%~dp1gfx\monarchs' -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant(); Write-Host ('Payload SHA-256: '+$h); if($h -eq 'fc6ec025b782c811636a0efb65a7b3f192f09fffd0ff6ca8051ef8bc6113db4e'){Write-Host 'PAYLOAD RESULT: CORRECT' -ForegroundColor Green}else{Write-Host 'PAYLOAD RESULT: WRONG CONTENT' -ForegroundColor Red; exit 1}"
if errorlevel 1 goto :payloadfail
echo.
echo ============================================================
echo V9 FEAT-REHYDRATION PATCH COMPLETE
echo Executable: %~f1
echo Expected V9 SHA-256:
echo 61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687
echo.
echo Test: quit completely, relaunch, Load/Continue, check feats.
echo ============================================================
pause
exit /b 0
:patchfail
echo.
echo PATCH FAILED. Nothing else will be changed.
pause
exit /b 1
:payloadfail
echo.
echo The executable was patched, but the existing payload failed its check.
pause
exit /b 1

RUN_APPLY_CK2_MJ_V9.bat (primary deliverable):
bat

@echo off
setlocal
title CK2 Monarch's Journey - Apply V9 (double-click me)
rem ================================================================
rem  Double-click this file.
rem  It finds your CK2game.exe, applies the V9 patch, makes a backup,
rem  and keeps this window open so you can read the result.
rem  Requirements: patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1 must be in this same folder.
rem ================================================================
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "EXE=%~1"
rem --- 1) use the file you dragged onto this window ---
if defined EXE goto :found
rem --- 2) look in the usual places ---
if exist "C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "C:\Program Files\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=C:\Program Files\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "D:\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=D:\Steam\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
if exist "E:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe" set "EXE=E:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Crusader Kings II\game\CK2game.exe"
if defined EXE goto :found
rem --- 3) ask the user to pick the file ---
echo.
echo I could not find CK2game.exe in the usual places.
echo A file-picker window will open now - choose your CK2game.exe.
echo (If nothing opens, drag CK2game.exe onto this BAT instead.)
echo.
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%P in (`powershell -NoProfile -Command "Add-Type -AssemblyName System.Windows.Forms; $f=New-Object System.Windows.Forms.OpenFileDialog; $f.Title='Select your CK2game.exe (May 2020 build)'; if($f.ShowDialog() -eq [System.Windows.Forms.DialogResult]::OK){$f.FileName}"`) do set "EXE=%%P"
if defined EXE goto :found
echo No file was chosen. Nothing was changed.
pause
exit /b 1
:found
if exist "%EXE%" goto :haveexe
echo ERROR: "%EXE%" does not exist.
pause
exit /b 1
:haveexe
if exist "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" goto :runpatch
echo ERROR: patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1 must be in the same folder as this BAT.
pause
exit /b 1
:runpatch
echo.
echo Found: %EXE%
echo.
echo This will change that file to V9. A verified backup is made first.
echo The game must not be running while this runs.
echo.
pause
powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%HERE%patch_ck2_mj_v9.ps1" Apply "%EXE%"
if errorlevel 1 goto :patchfail
rem --- payload check (gfx\monarchs) ---
for %%D in ("%EXE%") do set "GAMEDIR=%%~dpD"
if exist "%GAMEDIR%gfx\monarchs" goto :payloadok
echo ERROR: Payload not found at "%GAMEDIR%gfx\monarchs"
pause
exit /b 1
:payloadok
powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command "$h=(Get-FileHash -LiteralPath '%GAMEDIR%gfx\monarchs' -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant(); Write-Host ('Payload SHA-256: '+$h); if($h -eq 'fc6ec025b782c811636a0efb65a7b3f192f09fffd0ff6ca8051ef8bc6113db4e'){Write-Host 'PAYLOAD RESULT: CORRECT' -ForegroundColor Green}else{Write-Host 'PAYLOAD RESULT: WRONG CONTENT' -ForegroundColor Red; exit 1}"
if errorlevel 1 goto :payloadfail
echo.
echo ============================================================
echo V9 FEAT-REHYDRATION PATCH COMPLETE
echo Executable: %EXE%
echo Expected V9 SHA-256:
echo 61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687
echo.
echo Test: quit CK2 completely, relaunch, Load/Continue, check feats.
echo ============================================================
pause
exit /b 0
:patchfail
echo.
echo PATCH FAILED. Nothing else was changed.
echo Take a photo of this window and send it over.
pause
exit /b 1
:payloadfail
echo.
echo The executable was patched, but the payload check failed.
echo Take a photo of this window and send it over.
pause
exit /b 1

REVERT_CK2_MJ_V9_TO_V8.bat (goto style):
bat

@echo off
setlocal
title Revert CK2 Monarch's Journey V9 to V8

pause

pause
exit /b 1

echo.

echo 94d6fb403b4541a53f846b348722ee81bc832b66ac853f6fd532f08e2e8b7e93
pause
exit /b 0
:revertfail
echo REVERT FAILED. Stop and send a screenshot.
pause
exit /b 1

powershell

$exe = 'C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe'
$v7 = '57b18e4392d03f0a3a67bc2c8c8d643302a9c44a141d90000219051adc521571'
$v8 = '94d6fb403b4541a53f846b348722ee81bc832b66ac853f6fd532f08e2e8b7e93'
$v9 = '61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687'
$size = 24753368
function Sha([string]$p){ (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $p -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $exe)) { Write-Host "NOT FOUND: $exe" -ForegroundColor Red; exit }
$s = Sha $exe
$item = Get-Item -LiteralPath $exe
if ($item.Length -ne $size) { Write-Host "WRONG SIZE: $($item.Length) (expected $size)"; exit }
if ($s -eq $v9) { Write-Host 'Already V9 — nothing to do.' -ForegroundColor Green; exit }
if ($s -ne $v7 -and $s -ne $v8) { Write-Host "UNKNOWN FILE: $s"; exit }
Write-Host "Current state: $(if ($s -eq $v7) {'V7'} else {'V8'})"
$stamp = Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd_HHmmss'
$bak = "$exe.before_v9_$stamp.bak"
Copy-Item -LiteralPath $exe -Destination $bak
if ((Sha $bak) -ne $s) { Write-Host 'BACKUP VERIFY FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; exit }
Write-Host "Backup saved + verified: $bak"
$d = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($exe)
# V8 patches first if starting from V7
if ($s -eq $v7) {
  $p1 = 0x00666546; if (($d[$p1] -ne 0x74) -or ($d[$p1+1] -ne 0x0d)) { Write-Host 'Pre-check 1 failed'; exit }
  $d[$p1]=0x90; $d[$p1+1]=0x90
  $p2 = 0x007b786b; if (($d[$p2] -ne 0x75) -or ($d[$p2+1] -ne 0x05)) { Write-Host 'Pre-check 2 failed'; exit }
  $d[$p2]=0xeb; $d[$p2+1]=0x05
  Write-Host 'V8 bypasses applied.'
}
$p3 = 0x007b7906
if (($d[$p3] -ne 0xe8) -or ($d[$p3+1] -ne 0x85) -or ($d[$p3+2] -ne 0x71) -or ($d[$p3+3] -ne 0x8f) -or ($d[$p3+4] -ne 0xff)) { Write-Host 'Pre-check V9 failed'; exit }
$d[$p3]=0xb0; $d[$p3+1]=0x01; $d[$p3+2]=0x90; $d[$p3+3]=0x90; $d[$p3+4]=0x90
$msha = ShaBytes $d   # need in-memory hash helper

Let me write the final command text. Keep it tight but readable.
powershell

function Invoke-MJV9 {
    param([string]$Path)
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
    $v7 = '57b18e4392d03f0a3a67bc2c8c8d643302a9c44a141d90000219051adc521571'
    $v8 = '94d6fb403b4541a53f846b348722ee81bc832b66ac853f6fd532f08e2e8b7e93'
    $v9 = '61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687'
    function Sha([string]$p) { (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $p -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
    function ShaB([byte[]]$d) { $sha=[Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create(); try { (($sha.ComputeHash($d) | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString('x2') }) -join '') } finally { $sha.Dispose() } }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) { Write-Host "NOT FOUND: $Path" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $item = Get-Item -LiteralPath $Path
    if ($item.Length -ne 24753368) { Write-Host "WRONG SIZE: $($item.Length) bytes (expected 24753368). Is this the May-2020 3.3.3 game?" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $s = Sha $Path
    if ($s -eq $v9) { Write-Host 'Already V9. Nothing to do.' -ForegroundColor Green; return }
    if ($s -eq $v7) { $state = 'V7' } elseif ($s -eq $v8) { $state = 'V8' } else { Write-Host "UNKNOWN EXE: $s" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    Write-Host "Current state: $state. Applying V9..."
    $bak = "$Path.before_v9_" + (Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd_HHmmss') + '.bak'
    Copy-Item -LiteralPath $Path -Destination $bak
    if ((Sha $bak) -ne $s) { Write-Host 'BACKUP VERIFICATION FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    Write-Host "Backup saved and verified: $bak"
    $d = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($Path)
    if ($state -eq 'V7') {
        if ($d[0x00666546] -ne 0x74 -or $d[0x00666547] -ne 0x0d) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x00666546' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
        $d[0x00666546] = 0x90; $d[0x00666547] = 0x90
        if ($d[0x007b786b] -ne 0x75 -or $d[0x007b786c] -ne 0x05) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x007b786b' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
        $d[0x007b786b] = 0xeb; $d[0x007b786c] = 0x05
        Write-Host 'V8 bypasses applied.'
    }
    if ($d[0x007b7906] -ne 0xe8 -or $d[0x007b7907] -ne 0x85 -or $d[0x007b7908] -ne 0x71 -or $d[0x007b7909] -ne 0x8f -or $d[0x007b790a] -ne 0xff) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x007b7906 (V9 site)' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $d[0x007b7906] = 0xb0; $d[0x007b7907] = 0x01; $d[0x007b7908] = 0x90; $d[0x007b7909] = 0x90; $d[0x007b790a] = 0x90
    $msha = ShaB $d
    if ($msha -ne $v9) { Write-Host "INTERNAL HASH MISMATCH: $msha" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $tmp = "$Path.tmp"
    [IO.File]::WriteAllBytes($tmp, $d)
    if ((Sha $tmp) -ne $v9) { Write-Host 'TEMP FILE VERIFY FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; Remove-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Force; return }
    Copy-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Destination $Path -Force
    Remove-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Force
    if ((Sha $Path) -ne $v9) { Write-Host 'FINAL FILE VERIFY FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'RESULT: V9 PATCH APPLIED AND VERIFIED' -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "SHA-256: $v9"
    $gfx = Join-Path (Split-Path $Path -Parent) 'gfx\monarchs'
    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $gfx) {
        $ph = Sha $gfx
        Write-Host "Payload SHA-256: $ph"
        if ($ph -eq 'fc6ec025b782c811636a0efb65a7b3f192f09fffd0ff6ca8051ef8bc6113db4e') { Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: CORRECT' -ForegroundColor Green } else { Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: WRONG CONTENT' -ForegroundColor Red }
    } else {
        Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: gfx\monarchs not found next to the exe' -ForegroundColor Yellow
    }
    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'Test now: quit CK2 completely, relaunch, Load/Continue, check feats.'
}
Invoke-MJV9 'C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe'

Wait — one bug: in PS, array indexing on byte array with a computed expression like $d[0x007b7906] works. And comparisons chained with -or — need parentheses for clarity: if ($d[0x00666546] -ne 0x74 -or $d[0x00666547] -ne 0x0d) — operator precedence: -ne binds tighter than -or, so it evaluates ($d[0x00666546] -ne 0x74) -or ($d[0x00666547] -ne 0x0d). That's correct.

Also $item.Length — FileInfo.Length is long; compare with int 24753368 fine.

[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes with byte[] — good.

PowerShell

function Invoke-MJV9 {
    param([string]$Path)
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
    $v7 = '57b18e4392d03f0a3a67bc2c8c8d643302a9c44a141d90000219051adc521571'
    $v8 = '94d6fb403b4541a53f846b348722ee81bc832b66ac853f6fd532f08e2e8b7e93'
    $v9 = '61e4345ba1395f09d26f84bf030ae0474fce3f0635a3516edea56b46c486d687'
    function Sha([string]$p) { (Get-FileHash -LiteralPath $p -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() }
    function ShaB([byte[]]$d) { $sha=[Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create(); try { (($sha.ComputeHash($d) | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString('x2') }) -join '') } finally { $sha.Dispose() } }

    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) { Write-Host "NOT FOUND: $Path" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $item = Get-Item -LiteralPath $Path
    if ($item.Length -ne 24753368) { Write-Host "WRONG SIZE: $($item.Length) bytes (expected 24753368). Is this the May-2020 3.3.3 game?" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $s = Sha $Path
    if ($s -eq $v9) { Write-Host 'Already V9. Nothing to do.' -ForegroundColor Green; return }
    if ($s -eq $v7) { $state = 'V7' } elseif ($s -eq $v8) { $state = 'V8' } else { Write-Host "UNKNOWN EXE: $s" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    Write-Host "Current state: $state. Applying V9..."

    $bak = "$Path.before_v9_" + (Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd_HHmmss') + '.bak'
    Copy-Item -LiteralPath $Path -Destination $bak
    if ((Sha $bak) -ne $s) { Write-Host 'BACKUP VERIFICATION FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    Write-Host "Backup saved and verified: $bak"

    $d = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($Path)
    if ($state -eq 'V7') {
        if ($d[0x00666546] -ne 0x74 -or $d[0x00666547] -ne 0x0d) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x00666546' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
        $d[0x00666546] = 0x90; $d[0x00666547] = 0x90
        if ($d[0x007b786b] -ne 0x75 -or $d[0x007b786c] -ne 0x05) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x007b786b' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
        $d[0x007b786b] = 0xeb; $d[0x007b786c] = 0x05
        Write-Host 'V8 bypasses applied.'
    }
    if ($d[0x007b7906] -ne 0xe8 -or $d[0x007b7907] -ne 0x85 -or $d[0x007b7908] -ne 0x71 -or $d[0x007b7909] -ne 0x8f -or $d[0x007b790a] -ne 0xff) { Write-Host 'Pre-check failed at 0x007b7906 (V9 site)' -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $d[0x007b7906] = 0xb0; $d[0x007b7907] = 0x01; $d[0x007b7908] = 0x90; $d[0x007b7909] = 0x90; $d[0x007b790a] = 0x90

    $msha = ShaB $d
    if ($msha -ne $v9) { Write-Host "INTERNAL HASH MISMATCH: $msha" -ForegroundColor Red; return }
    $tmp = "$Path.tmp"
    [IO.File]::WriteAllBytes($tmp, $d)
    if ((Sha $tmp) -ne $v9) { Write-Host 'TEMP FILE VERIFY FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; Remove-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Force; return }
    Copy-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Destination $Path -Force
    Remove-Item -LiteralPath $tmp -Force
    if ((Sha $Path) -ne $v9) { Write-Host 'FINAL FILE VERIFY FAILED' -ForegroundColor Red; return }

    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'RESULT: V9 PATCH APPLIED AND VERIFIED' -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "SHA-256: $v9"
    $gfx = Join-Path (Split-Path $Path -Parent) 'gfx\monarchs'
    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $gfx) {
        $ph = Sha $gfx
        Write-Host "Payload SHA-256: $ph"
        if ($ph -eq 'fc6ec025b782c811636a0efb65a7b3f192f09fffd0ff6ca8051ef8bc6113db4e') { Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: CORRECT' -ForegroundColor Green } else { Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: WRONG CONTENT' -ForegroundColor Red }
    } else {
        Write-Host 'PAYLOAD: gfx\monarchs not found next to the exe' -ForegroundColor Yellow
    }
    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'Test now: quit CK2 completely, relaunch, Load/Continue, check feats.'
}
Invoke-MJV9 'C:\Users\UZWERG\Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe'

What it does:

    If your game is already V9 → it says "Already V9" and stops.
    Otherwise it makes a verified backup (CK2game.exe.before_v9_….bak next to the game), applies the patch, and checks the result fingerprint is exactly 61e4345b…
    It also checks the gfx\monarchs payload and tells you CORRECT or WRONG.

Notes:

    Make sure CK2 is fully closed before running it.
    If your game is somewhere else (not Desktop\SteamCrusader\CK2game.exe), change the path on the very last line to where your CK2game.exe is.
    It never deletes or redistributes anything — it only edits your own file.

I've also saved this same command to ALL-MY-LOGS-SO-FAR/05_patches_and_scripts/ps1/RUN_APPLY_CK2_MJ_V9_INLINE.ps1 in the workspace, so it's not lost if you want to copy it from there later.
